On the assembly side: attaching both ends of a dragged structural member to existing ports (closed structures — this needs the member's own length to match a port-to-port distance the drag itself doesn't know until the far end is also snapped, so today a member only ever grows a fresh node from a free port), adding or removing a primitive within a cross-section (the section editor edits an existing primitive's shape and phase but not the primitive count, since the glued-face-length rule such an edit would have to keep is not yet settled), and live visual feedback on the edited target's own rendered hull before a session is confirmed — an added part or member is only ever shown as the drag's own ghost; the base/docked-vessel/draft's real mesh is not rebuilt until `commitAssembly` applies the session, so a multi-step edit has to be held in the player's head (or read off the panel's own shelf/selection state) rather than seen on the ship itself. See `memos/mikanixonable/dev.md` for design direction.

## Project concept (from memos/mikanixonable/dev.md)

An orbital-mechanics shooting game, intended as a web game, set in the Earth-Moon system (current build: LEO only):

- Gameplay centers on realistic orbital maneuvers and physically accurate ballistic calculations, low-poly but visually appealing depictions of Earth's atmosphere, spacecraft, and spacecraft destruction.
- Orbital calculations should account for Sun-Earth-Moon three-body dynamics, atmospheric drag, and tidal forces. Reference concepts to consider: Sun-Earth and Earth-Moon Lagrange points, sun-synchronous recurrent orbits, halo orbits, low-energy transfers and ballistic capture, double lunar swingbys, and gradual altitude-raising approach maneuvers (like the Kounotori HTV's ISS approach).
- Aesthetic goals: Earth auroras, auroras from solar storms, auroras from charged particles generated by combat, shadows on Earth's evening clouds, and the Dzhanibekov effect on destroyed debris.

## Controls (current build)

**Translation is body-frame, not orbital-frame** (`VesselThrottle.buildThrust` builds a direction in body axes and rotates it by `att.q`): W/S = nose-forward/aft (+Z/−Z), A/D = lateral (+X/−X), Q/E = vertical (+Y/−Y). All six fire at the same selected magnitude `THROTTLE_LEVELS[throttleIdx]` (4 stages — [1]/[2]/[3]/[4], the 4th at 4× the 3rd's output — no "cutoff" stage; thrust exists only while a direction key is held).

Rotation: I/K = pitch, **L/J = yaw**, **O/U = roll** (signs per `VesselThrottle.updateTorque`'s `inX`/`inY`/`inZ`; `RcsEffects.sync` lights the nozzles whose own torque points the same way as that commanded torque, so the visible thrusters and the key held can't disagree). T = RCS rotation damping toggle, **F = prograde attitude reset** (one-shot: only sets the hold ON), **C = prograde-hold autopilot toggle** (PD controller torquing the nose to the velocity vector; any manual rotation key cancels it), **V = fine-attitude toggle**. **`Digit9`/`Digit0`** (the top-row 9/0) toggle the `up`(left/+X)/`down`(right/-X) radiator panels' deploy state (`Vessel.handleEdgePress` calls `radiator.toggle('up'|'down')` directly — the same panels the status-window buttons drive) — distinct from the numeric-keypad `Numpad0`/`Numpad1` below, which roll the camera instead.

G = toggle whether the chase camera's reference frame follows ship attitude vs. the orbital frame (see below; combat view only — the key isn't read at all on the map). Z (hold) = gunsight zoom. Space = fire. **Right click on an enemy or another self-flown ship** (within the same `CLICK_MOVE_THRESHOLD` click-vs-drag test the left button uses) **opens the same property window the map's own right click would** (`MapContextActions.handleCombatRightClick`, via `Targeter.pickTargetAt`) — its item list includes "ターゲットに設定"/"第二ターゲットに設定" (`MapContextActions.combatTargetLockItems`, combat-view only) alongside the usual フォーカス/航法ターゲット items; a miss instead falls through to the shared empty-space menu offering "設定メニューを開く" (the same pause menu `[Esc]` opens). Target selection has no automatic re-pick: whichever `Vessel`/`Vessel` was last set as target/secondary stays until explicitly cleared or replaced, even off-screen or behind the camera. The secondary target only affects what's drawn (cyan orbit line/marker/enemy-list row) — board marks, ⇨/⇦ bearing markers and LEAD all read the primary target only. **left drag**, **right drag**, or **arrow keys** = camera yaw/pitch, **`Numpad0`/`Numpad1`** (alt: `/`/`_`) = camera roll (around the camera's own current view axis — `ChaseCamera` in combat view, `MapCamera` on the map, where it rolls the map's up vector), wheel or two-finger pinch = zoom distance, **middle drag** or **`@`/`:`/`;`/`]`** (up/down/left/right) = pan — the keyboard pan keys synthesize `panDx`/`panDy` at `C.CAM_KEY_PAN_RATE` px/s in `CameraSystem.update` so they drive the exact same `ChaseCamera`/`MapCamera` pan code path as the middle-drag, a keyboard alternative for laptops without a usable middle-click drag. `,` / `.` = time warp (thrust, guns, enemy fire and contact physics are all gated above ×`MAX_PHYS_SIM_SPEED` = 4 via `SimSpeedManager.can*`). M = orbit-planning map, N = auto-warp to the next node, X = delete selected node / discard the whole plan, H = help, R = manual reload (after the game ends it instead re-launches **the same stage** — `Launcher.restart` discards the running `Game` and constructs a fresh one for `launchedStage`, so a launch reached without a `?stage=` param still restarts the stage actually played rather than dropping back to the stage-select screen). A decided stage stops the simulation underneath its result screen: `Game.update` gates `advanceSimulation` on `!isPaused && activeStage.isPlaying`, so integration, every ship's `updateControls` and each concrete stage's own `update` all stop at the decision. What keeps running outside that gate is the display side — the camera, the display window, prediction growth and pointer input — since a frozen camera would leave the render origin behind in absolute ECI and everything on screen would drift out of frame at orbital speed.
On the map, **left-clicking a self-flown ship or a base selects it** (`MapContextActions.handleLeftClick`, checked before a left click can instead place/select a maneuver node) — a ship opens its property window, a base becomes the selected one (`Docking.selectBase`, hinted on screen); neither switches the view or the operated ship by itself. **A single click never commits anything that can't be taken back**: switching which ship is flown, opening a base's operations and starting an assembly session are reachable only through an explicit property-window item or a double-click. **Double-clicking any pickable object moves the overview camera's focus there, and additionally makes a self-flown ship the operated one** (`MapContextActions.handleDoubleClick`, over the whole `pickables` set — celestial bodies/ships/bases/ammo/apsis/AN-DN icons alike); it consumes `Input`'s native-`dblclick`-backed `takeDoubleClicks` queue and runs independently of the single left-click handling above, so a double-click on a ship both opens its window (from the two constituent single clicks) and focuses/activates it (from the double-click itself). A body whose label is currently hidden — by label-collision relaxation or by a body in front of it — is not a double-click candidate, since there is nothing on screen there to have aimed at.

On the map, right-clicking a celestial label, a living ship, a base, an ammo pickup, a periapsis/apoapsis icon, or a relative AN/DN icon opens a **property window** for that object (`hud/property-window.ts`'s `PropertyWindow`, opened/owned by `MapContextActions` — see `map-context-actions.ts` above): a title/subtitle, a live-updating property block (armor/temp/power/ammo for a self-flown ship; distance/closing speed/relative speed/orbital elements/relative inclination for an enemy; money/docked-ship count/distance/orbital elements for a base; distance/orbital elements for an ammo pickup; display name/kind/μ/radius/distance/orbital elements for a celestial body; altitude/time-to-crossing for an apsis or relative AN/DN icon), and the same action items `MapContextActions.itemsFor` always built (by `MapPickable.kind`): every kind offers "move focus" (`Game.frameControls.setFocus`); a ship additionally offers "set/clear navigation target" (`NavTarget.toggleTarget` — independent of `Targeter`'s combat target, see the Architecture section); an apsis or relative-AN/DN icon additionally offers "add node here" (`PlanEditor.addNodeAt`, at that icon's crossing time) and, for relative AN/DN only, "warp here" (`SimSpeedManager.startAutoWarpTo`); a base additionally offers "基地の操作を開く" (`Docking.openBaseOperations` — the 格納艦艇/部品/生産 window) and "組み立てる" (`Docking.startAssembly` — the part shelf, which pauses the game while it is open), two windows over the world rather than a view to enter, alongside docking/undocking and resource transfer with the operated ship. On a stage whose `authoring` is set (CREATIVE today), a self-flown ship/enemy/ammo pickup/base additionally offers "duplicate" — opens `ObjectPlacerPanel` pre-filled with that object's current orbital elements (基準天体/近地点+遠地点高度/傾斜角/Ω/ω/ν all copied — ν included, so the duplicate starts exactly where the original is; nothing offsets it), object type fixed to match, ready to place a lightly-tweaked copy. A self-flown ship or a base additionally shows a ✎ rename button beside the window's title (its `GameEntity.name`).

A property window can be dragged by its header, and its 📌 button **clips** it — an unclipped window is a *temporary* one: at most one exists at a time (opening or right-clicking a second object closes the previous temporary window), and it closes on an outside click, on picking any action item, or on leaving the map view. A clipped window survives all of those, so several can be pinned open side by side to compare objects; only its own ✕ button, deleting the object, or the object itself vanishing (destroyed/picked up/removed) closes it. Right-clicking an object that already has an open window (of either kind) just moves that window to the click position and brings it to front, rather than opening a second one. Right-clicking empty space still opens a plain context menu (`ContextMenu`, not a property window — there's no object to show properties of) offering "show orbit object window" — a top-right list of the same candidate set, click to focus, right-click to open that object's property window (`hud/object-list-panel.ts`'s `ObjectListPanel`) — and "open settings menu" (`MapContextActions`'s constructor-injected `pauseMenu.toggle(true)` — the same pause menu `[Esc]` opens) — on every stage; a stage whose `authoring` is set (CREATIVE today) additionally offers "place object" there (`ObjectPlacerPanel`, below).

Esc (or the ⚙ gear) = pause menu, owned by `hud/pause-menu.ts`: `PauseMenu.toggle` fires `onPauseMenuOpenChange`, which `main.ts` syncs to `launcher.current?.pause()`/`.resume()` (so every open/close path — Esc, gear click, [閉じる] — pauses/resumes whichever `Game` is currently running), plus a BGM volume slider and a "quit to title" button (`onQuitToTitle` → `Launcher.returnToTitle()`, which discards the running `Game`, shows the title (stage-select) screen again, and starts whichever stage is chosen there). While paused `Game.update` returns early, so HP auto-regen (`HP_REGEN_RATE` = 1 HP/s, inside `Vessel.updateControls`) can't be farmed from the menu. **Esc has exactly one owner**: `Game.handleInput` takes it off `Input`'s edge queue and hands it to `hud/overlay-manager.ts`'s `OverlayManager`, which closes whichever registered overlay is topmost (a property window, a base's operations or assembly window, the save browser, help) and opens the pause menu only once nothing was open to close — `handleInput` still runs before the pause check, so this dispatch (and every other key's owner) decides for itself what a paused frame means; `ViewManager.handleInput` leaves `[M]` unconsumed while a dock is open.

F3 = open/close the load-inspection window (`perf-meter.ts` above; the `[Esc]` menu's 負荷を表示 item is the same entry point). It is non-modal — the game keeps running and is not paused — and closes only through F3 or its own ✕ (it registers with `OverlayManager` with no `tempWindowGroup`, so ESC/outside-click never close it). F5 = clip the current moment as a named snapshot, F9 = open/close the snapshot browser (`save/` + `hud/save-browser.ts` above; the `[Esc]` menu's スナップショット item is the same entry point) — both keys are `src/snapshot-controls.ts`'s `SnapshotControls`, not `Game`; closing the browser via `[Esc]` is instead `hud/overlay-manager.ts`'s `OverlayManager` (see `game.ts`'s `handleInput` above), which dispatches Escape to whichever registered overlay is topmost. The browser and the pause menu both register under `exclusiveGroup: 'system-modal'`, so opening one closes the other, and `SaveBrowser.open()`/`close()` pause and resume the game themselves rather than leaving that to whoever opened them. F5 is refused (with a hint, nothing written) once the active stage has decided (`!activeStage.isPlaying`), since a decided `phase` restores as plain data with no `StageResult` and would otherwise resume play with no way to ever see the result screen it should have shown. Autosave takes an unnamed, unclipped snapshot every 60 real seconds on the same condition. **Loading a clipped or automatic snapshot discards the running `Game` and constructs its replacement**, not a page reload: double-clicking its card in the browser calls `Launcher.loadSnapshot(id)`, so the running `Game` a load lands in was constructed with that state from the start rather than having it swapped in mid-session.

Firing implies fine-attitude authority (`FINE_ATTITUDE_SCALE` = 0.5): `Vessel.updateTorque` passes `fineAttitude || fire.isFiring`, so releasing the trigger restores full authority with no edge-tracking state.

### The CREATIVE stage

CREATIVE (`stage-dictionary.ts`'s `CreativeStage`, `StageId = 'creative'`) is a stage with the fewest restrictions rather than a second axis alongside the campaign stages — it sits at the tail of `STAGE_CLASSES` and reads on the title screen's クリエイティブモード tab (`Stage.selectGroup`) exactly like `stage0.ts`/`stage1.ts` read on ステージモード. Launched from that tab, or `?stage=creative`, it starts `ViewManager`'s initial view at `'map'` (the map is where placement happens, and a launch with zero ships starts there regardless — see `game.ts` above — since combat view has no meaning without one to fly). It is not a special case layered onto an otherwise single-ship game: 0..n self-flown ships is the general form `EntityManager`/`Simulator`/`Predictor`/`ContactPhysics`/`ActiveVesselController` already treat uniformly, and a campaign stage's "exactly 1 ship, lose on its destruction" is the specialization — `CreativeStage` not overriding `init` at all (so a launch places nothing and starts with no ship) and its instance `executesPlans = true`/`authoring = this` (a ship may fly its own plan, and objects may be placed/duplicated) are the only declarations that distinguish this stage `Stage` exposes (see `stage.ts` above) — `Game` itself, and every consumer of those capabilities, reads only the capability flags and never branches on which stage is running; a destroyed ship's slot is reclaimed the same way it is on every stage, via `ActiveVesselController.reclaimDead()`. `[M]` is disabled — `ViewManager.handleInput` refuses to leave map for combat, with a hint, whenever `ActiveVesselController.current` is `null` — until at least one placed ship is active, since the combat view has no meaning without a ship to fly. The マップモード-only 艦艇配置 panel (`creative/object-placer-panel.ts`) places ships by orbital elements or by a Lagrange-point halo/Lissajous orbit (see `src/physics/halo.ts` above for why the latter drifts once the simulation runs); a placed ship's right-click menu (`kind: 'player'` in `MapContextActions.itemsFor`) opens with 軌道計画の実行 — a single item cycling OFF→瞬間移動→自動操縦→OFF on each click (`keepOpen: true`, so the window stays open across clicks; label shows the current mode, `plan/plan-executor.ts`'s `PlanExecutionMode` above), shown only while `activeStage.executesPlans` — a stage with no driver for `PlanExecutor`/the `'instant'` teleport would offer a control with no effect) — followed by 操作対象にする/操作対象を解除 (`activeVessels.set`/`setOrNull(null)`, one label replacing the other by whether this ship is already active — deactivating leaves `Game.player` `null`, the same "no ship flown" state a total wipe-out on this stage leaves), フォーカスを移動, 航法ターゲットに設定/解除 (`NavTarget`, same `navTargetItems` helper the `'body'`/`'ship'` kinds use), and 削除 — the last is simply absent from the menu for the currently-active ship (deleting it would leave `player` dangling).

The orbital direction markers (`vessel/pilot-markers.ts`) deliberately carry no key hints in their labels: combat-view translation is body-frame, so none of those six directions has a key. The keys that *do* mean prograde/normal/radial are the map-mode Δv edit keys, and the direction markers are hidden there.

Chase camera reference frame (`camFollowAttitude` on `ChaseCamera`, default **on**, toggled via `[G]`, which `CombatCameraSystem.update` reads directly and forwards as `chaseCamera.toggleFollowAttitude(player)` — so it's only live while combat view is active): the third-person camera's orientation is stored as a single quaternion offset (`rot`) from a reference frame, but *which* frame is configurable. ON (default) references the ship's own attitude (`player.att.q`), so rotating the ship with I/K/J/L/U/O visibly swings the whole camera (including its roll) right along with it, RCS-linked. OFF references ECI directly, so the camera holds a fixed orientation in inertial space regardless of how the ship rotates, until dragged. Toggling reinterprets `rot` through the ship's current attitude so the view doesn't jump. While the ship is destroyed, ON stops folding in the ship's attitude (which would otherwise be its post-destruction tumble) and holds the last orientation instead. `CombatCameraSystem` itself only decides between `ChaseCamera` and `GunsightCamera` (via `zoomActive`) and animates the FOV each holds in its own `view`.

Ammo system: guns fire alternately from the two front bores and consume magazine rounds (`MAG_ROUNDS`=32/mag, `INITIAL_MAGS`=3 linked at start, `MAGS_PER_BARREL`=3 consumed before a barrel-swap reload — doubling the per-mag capacity from an original 16-round design let the visible chain be roughly half as long while carrying comparable total ammo). The belt of unused mags hangs off the ship's port (+X) side as child meshes of `player.obj`, and the belt slides continuously into the hull as rounds are fired (`beltFeed` = 1 − rounds/`MAG_ROUNDS` offsets the anchor by up to one pitch; on mag consumption the feed wraps 1→0 exactly when the link count drops, so the motion reads as seamless). The head link sits half-inside the hull. `BeltPhysics.update` (in `vessel/belt-physics.ts`) simulates the belt's sag as an actual physics chain, not a canned animation: link joints are point masses (`beltPos`/`beltPrevPos`, Verlet-integrated in the ship's body frame) connected by rigid-rod distance constraints (position-based dynamics, 6 relaxation iterations per frame) to `MAG_BELT_PITCH`. Since the ship is in free fall, ordinary gravity can't sag anything (equivalence principle — belt and hull fall together); the only real driver is the fictitious force felt in the ship's non-inertial (accelerating/rotating) frame: `-a_thrust - α×r - ω×(ω×r) - 2ω×v` (translational inertia, Euler, centrifugal, Coriolis), built from `thrustAccelVec` (world-frame thrust accel, rotated into body frame, passed in each call from `Vessel.updateControls` via `Belt.update`) and `player.att.w` (with `α` estimated by finite-differencing `w` against `prevShipW`, both tracked inside `BeltPhysics`). When the player isn't thrusting or rotating, the chain just hangs straight — no ambient wobble is faked in.

Each link's *orientation* is derived separately from its position, via parallel transport: `qBend = qDelta * qPrev` where `qDelta` is the minimal (zero-twist) rotation taking the previous link's local +X to this link's tangent — so bending (the chain folding link-to-link) is unconstrained by construction, matching a real ammo belt's easy up/down/left/right flex. Roll (twist about the link's own +X, the one DOF parallel transport leaves undefined) is instead tracked explicitly per link in `beltTwist[]`, driven by the ship's own roll rate (`player.att.w.z * MAG_CHAIN_ROLL_GAIN`) at link 0 and propagated down the chain with a one-frame lag (`prevTwist`) and exponential smoothing (`MAG_CHAIN_ROLL_RATE`), clamped every frame to `±MAG_CHAIN_MAX_ROLL_DEG` — so however it's driven, twist can never exceed that bound. The final per-link quaternion is `qBend * qTwist(local +X)`.

`ContactPhysics` (in `game/simulation/contact.ts`; rigid-sphere contact for casings/enemies/pickups/radiator folds/belt) is driven from `Simulator.advance` in two cadences: `resolveSubstep` runs once per substep alongside the integration, while `resolveBelt` runs once per frame at the real (unwarped) `dt`, since the belt is a ship-local simulation on its own real-time cadence. Both are gated on `simSpeedManager.canResolvePhysicalCollisions` — at higher warp contact is skipped entirely, since substeps up to 20s each make rigid-body contact resolution meaningless. The ship's contact and hit radius are the one `PLAYER_HULL_RADIUS` (real hull size, 2.6m), so a freshly-ejected casing (spawned ~2.9m from the hull) clears it rather than being flung outward on the same frame. A casing/hull impact plays `WorldSfx.clank()` from the colliding entity's own `collideWith` (`DebrisPiece` for the casing side, `Vessel` for the hull side), not from the contact resolver.

When mags run low (`LOGISTICS_LOW_MAGS`), resupply pickups spawn phase-shifted 1.25–2.5 km ahead on the player's own orbit (`stage-utils/logistics.ts`'s `Logistics.spawnForPlayer`; ▣ 弾薬 marker, absorb within `AMMO_PICKUP_RADIUS`). Casings use a CIWS-style bottleneck profile (rim/extractor groove/tapered body/shoulder/neck via `LatheGeometry`) with **low** ejection speed but widely varying per-casing spin, on an asymmetric inertia tensor so the Dzhanibekov effect shows on tumbling casings the same way it does on debris. Spin-up SFX is filtered-noise friction (no oscillator). Vessel ships are modeled at `ENEMY_SCALE` (20×) via `obj.scale.setScalar(...)` with `ENEMY_RADIUS` (180 m) matching for hit detection; their destruction VFX (`spawnFlash` sizes, `scatterFragments` size range) scale by the same factor so the explosion reads proportionate to the larger hull.

Mouse buttons are swapped from the naive default: **left button drags the camera; a left click that moves less than `CLICK_MOVE_THRESHOLD` (6px) is instead queued as a click** (in map mode: selects the self-flown ship/base it lands on — see `MapContextActions.handleLeftClick` — or, failing that, places/selects a maneuver node) — see `Input.clicks()`. **Right button also drags the camera** (feeding the same `dx`/`dy` as the left drag — a two-finger trackpad drag, since right-click has no natural trackpad equivalent), and a right click within `CLICK_MOVE_THRESHOLD` (`Input.pushIfClick`, shared across left/middle/right) instead opens a target/context menu. Firing is Space-only now — right click no longer fires. This lets map-mode node placement and camera dragging share the left button, and camera dragging and menu-opening share the right button, without a modifier key.

Map mode ([M]) does not freeze physics: `Simulator.advance` runs every frame while `activeStage.isPlaying && !paused`, and neither firing nor manual rotation (RCS, IJKLUO) is gated by map mode — neither shares a key with Δv editing. Translation (WASDQE) goes to whichever owner claims it first: with no node selected, WASDQE fire the real engines exactly as in combat view; with one selected, `PlanEditor.updateEditing` claims those six keys through `Input.takeHeld` in the input phase, so they edit that node's Δv (prograde/normal/radial, not body axes) and `Vessel` sees them as un-pressed for the frame. So the player can fly for real while the map is open, and only loses direct thrust control while actively shaping a burn. Only the pointer dispatch is layered on top of `update`; the map-mode panels (表示 / PREDICT / 座標系 / 軌道オブジェクト) show and refresh themselves from their owners' `sync`. The map camera's drag yaw sign is intentionally opposite the combat chase camera's convention (`+=` vs `-=` on `mouse.dx`) so panning left/right reads the same in both views.

Each of the six translation keys can be **latched** by pressing it twice within `THRUST_LATCH_DOUBLE_TAP_SEC` (`VesselThrottle`'s `updateThrustLatches`/`isThrustHeld`): a latched key keeps firing that thruster as if held, independent of the physical key state, until double-tapped again to release it. This lets sustained burns run without holding a key down — useful in particular while the map is open and the mouse is busy dragging the camera/plan. Latches are per-key (not per-axis), cleared on `clearTransientState` (deactivating/parking the ship); holding a key's opposite direction also holds that key's latch cleared for as long as the opposite key stays down, so latching W and then holding S gives reverse thrust instead of the two canceling out, and releasing S leaves W's latch gone rather than reviving it. Above `MAX_PHYS_SIM_SPEED` (where thrust doesn't apply anyway) double-tapping neither latches nor consumes the press, so a latch can't accumulate silently during high warp and surprise-fire once warp drops back down. Holding **any** opposite pair together — W+S, A+D, or Q+E — is an emergency all-stop: it zeroes thrust on *every* axis (not just the held pair's own) and clears every latch, regardless of what else is held or latched at the time.
